**Prior 2026-08-22 update:** **DEBT-472 filed: the fakes-over-mocks rule sanctions unverified doubles at every external seam.** An owner question after DEBT-471 — how ~1,100 LOC of hand-rolled Stripe mocks coexist with a fakes-over-mocks mandate — was answered by a six-pass read-only census of 578 test files (4,061 unit / 398 browser / 258 integration cases) at `070cd567`. The cause is rule text, not agent drift: `.claude/rules/testing.md:27` and `AGENTS.md:537-541` prescribe inline `vi.fn()` objects for "external dependency (Drizzle db, Clerk, Stripe SDK)", a branch introduced by `792beed7` (2026-02-02), the guidance rewrite prompted by DEBT-051, with its example captioned "no fake exists" and never re-evaluated. ADR-003:161 labels `{ findById: vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(question) }` the canonical BAD mock while `AGENTS.md:565` labels the same construct ✅ OK under the name `fakeDb`; `.coderabbit.yaml:67` encodes the permissive reading; the two archived adjudications (DEBT-035 called the `RepoDb` chain a fake, DEBT-444 called it the anti-pattern) are invisible to agents. The canon's second clause — a fake must be verified against its real implementation by a contract test — exists for 2 of 24 fakes; `FakePaymentGateway` is a spy with no behavior and cannot diverge detectably; `FakeStripeCheckoutClient` has no executable contract against Stripe. Measured: 187 `as unknown as RepoDb` chain doubles across 23 files including five verified tautological filter tests in `drizzle-attempt-repository.test.ts:932-1055`; `DrizzleStripeEventRepository.pruneProcessedBefore` has zero integration coverage; in the Stripe doubles the omitted `has_more` routes `stripe-checkout-sessions.ts:198` into its fallback so the DEBT-470 tail-scan success path is exercised by one file; 17 own-code `vi.mock()` calls survive in `app/`+`components/` because no estate-wide sweep of that rule has ever run; DEBT-271's 2026-03-03 deletion of structural assertions was reversed the same day by `b420f147` because nothing guarded it. The estate is otherwise clean — zero tautological `expect(true)`, snapshots, `as any`, `.only`, swallowed errors, or always-true skips — so this is rule-compliant work, not gate evasion. [DEBT-472](../_archive/debt/debt-472-test-double-fidelity-and-contract-discipline.md) orders the fix rule-first: correct the rule text with a shape-versus-behavior test and one Meszaros-taxonomy definition set, add a multiline-safe source scan before any migration, mandate fake↔real contract tests by blast radius (`FakePaymentGateway` first), apply the census dispositions, re-scope DEBT-468 Parts 2–4 onto the fake-extension order with `stripe-checkout-sessions-live-retrieve.test.ts` as the zero-cost first migration, and record that resolutions which delete a pattern must land with the scan that keeps it deleted. The Active register is DEBT-337, DEBT-414, DEBT-464, DEBT-465, DEBT-468, DEBT-469, and DEBT-472. Next Debt ID is DEBT-473.

**Earlier 2026-08-20 update:** **DEBT-471 filed: required PR CI can fail on Stripe-owned UI and runner package mirrors unrelated to the diff.** A markdown-only PR (#811, one file, prose) supplied an exact unchanged-head comparison: `35dcca24` failed `E2E smoke` on 2026-08-19 and passed attempt 3 on 2026-08-20 with zero code changes. On attempt 2, Azure Ubuntu index requests stalled even after the fallback archive began responding; the installer ran 56m05s, emitted nothing for its final 53m45s, and never reached browser tests. [DEBT-471](../_archive/debt/debt-471-e2e-ci-external-fragility.md) corrects the first filing's proposed fixes: Stripe's own guidance says Checkout prevents automated testing, archived DEBT-205 records the same hosted-DOM break, and both the DEBT-415 no-card trial and PR #808 paid path depend on Stripe-owned selectors. Direct API provisioning is not equivalent hosted coverage. The chosen direction removes both hosted-DOM journeys from required PR CI while retaining separate repository-owned Checkout/session/sync/entitlement contracts and explicitly scheduled/manual hosted smokes if still valuable. The install fix adds a step-local bound and narrows the browser install to Chromium; Playwright explicitly advises against browser caching on Linux because OS dependencies are not cacheable. The audit also separates all networked Stripe test-mode calls from the DOM issue, corrects the healthy E2E runtime to 3m54s, keeps `cancel-in-progress` as a waste-limiting control, records five pushed-head workflow runs plus three attempts on the final head, and proves why Playwright 1.62.1's default HTML reporter can hold open a local retry-recovered pass. **Later 2026-08-14 update:** **DEBT-467 filed: trial setup Checkout replays a stale terminal Session URL.** The DEBT-466 spike's report-only finding is now a register item, after an owner-directed same-day code-path sweep. The setup path's deterministic key (`trial_setup_session:{userId}:{externalSubscriptionId}:{disclosureVersion}`) replays the saved `open` body and URL after the live Session becomes `complete` or is expired out-of-band, and the no-retrieve setup loop accepts it; natural expiry self-detects via the saved `expires_at` (Stripe default 24 hours), and open-Session replay is correct deduplication. Reachability is real: the trial banner's add-card button renders across the app when `inTrial`, `plan`, and `trialEndsAt` are present, with no payment-method or operation-state gate; the use case performs no setup-Session preflight, and the duplicate pending-operation insert is absorbed silently. A completed-setup re-click before the original Session's 24-hour expiry can therefore redirect to a non-usable Checkout URL. The fix design uses replay-faithful red tests and strict live post-create retrieval on primary and recovery creates: exhausted retrieval, id mismatch, or missing live status must fail closed rather than return or backfill from the cached create snapshot. It also re-decides the setup traversal bound with measured receipts, preserves consent-metadata/snapshot/mismatch invariants, and adds a dated follow-up ADR-015 §4 amendment once the license conditions hold. Implementation is sequenced after or with DEBT-466 Part A's constant split. The Active register is DEBT-337, DEBT-414, DEBT-464, DEBT-465, DEBT-466, and DEBT-467. Next Debt ID is DEBT-468.

**Earlier 2026-08-14 update:** **DEBT-466 owner design re-review is complete.** An isolated Stripe test-mode spike created a setup Session, replayed it, expired it, and replayed it again: create replay kept returning the saved `open` body and URL with `Idempotent-Replayed`, while live retrieve returned `expired`. Setup therefore does not share the subscription replay-chain mechanism and instead exposes a distinct stale-URL defect that was not absorbed or filed at that time (since filed as DEBT-467; see the DEBT-467 filing update above). The settled DEBT-466 design splits the constants: subscription replay traversal becomes 10; setup defective-response replacement remains 3. Measured cached-create/live-retrieve medians were 120.5/99.2 ms; under the repository wrapper's third-attempt-success envelope, primary plus 10 recoveries budgets 13.850 seconds, while 20 budgets 26.441 seconds before request preflight or reconciliation. The installed Stripe SDK's separate timeout/retry defaults mean the cap is explicitly a healthy-service planning bound, not an absolute timeout. ADR-015 §4 now records the narrow, conditioned BUG-245 subscription exception and withholds that license from the no-retrieve setup path. DEBT-466 remains Open for implementation. The Active register is DEBT-337, DEBT-414, DEBT-464, DEBT-465, and DEBT-466. Next Debt ID is DEBT-467.

**Earliest 2026-08-14 update:** **DEBT-466 execution audit corrected the checkout replay-chain design.** The live subscription defect remains confirmed: deterministic create keys replay saved responses, the adapter retrieves current Session state, and four retained completed Sessions occupy the primary plus three recovery keys, so the fifth same-(user, plan, variant) attempt fails with `checkout=error`. Stripe documents key pruning only after keys are at least 24 hours old, not an exact 24-hour window. The audit corrected the fourth/fifth-attempt arithmetic, the DEBT-410 citation (§B.11 item 8, not D8), caller-key ownership, and the false no-test-pin claim; the setup exhaustion test hardcodes four calls and attempts 1–3. That audit required owner re-review before implementation: the setup loop does no live retrieve, so Stripe's saved-response semantics did not establish the same replay chain there, while a subscription rung is create plus retrieve and the proposed cap 20 had no verified four-second bound against the route's 30-second budget. The planned implementation had to preserve BUG-245 determinism, measure latency, test subscription depth, adjudicate setup separately, record saved-response/at-least-24-hour semantics, and reconcile ADR-015 §4. The later update above records that completed review. The Active register is DEBT-337, DEBT-414, DEBT-464, DEBT-465, and DEBT-466. Next Debt ID is DEBT-467.

**Earlier 2026-08-13 update:** **DEBT-465 filed: advanced test-quality practices adoption (CRAP report, mutation testing, Gherkin acceptance tests, UI QA procedures).** An audit of the test estate (556 test files across the four lanes) found the suite broad but unaudited: no measure of whether tests constrain behavior (`grading.ts` carries 5 tests; `subscription-write-guard.ts` carries 21 table-driven cases nothing has audited for bite), no ranked under-tested-complexity triage (the renewal-notice delivery repository has zero direct repository unit tests; its behavior is covered in integration), no UI-independent business-rule specifications, and a long list of UI surfaces with route-level automation gaps (enumerated in `docs/dev/qa-procedures.md`). [ADR-019](../adr/adr-019-test-quality-practices.md) (Proposed) records the decision and the binding observational posture — no metric becomes a CI gate without a new ADR, extending the standing coverage-as-observational policy. Four canonical runbooks landed in `docs/dev/` (code-quality-metrics, mutation-testing, acceptance-testing, qa-procedures) plus the new `docs/qa/` register seeded with QA-001/QA-002 drafts; QA-001 is written to absorb stabilization-checklist Flows A–C once Active, while Flow D remains. Pilot scope is pinned: 8 mutation targets in domain/application, a zero-new-runner acceptance harness (`@amiceli/vitest-cucumber` in the unit lane) with 17 receipt-backed candidate rules, and a `scripts/crap-report.ts` design over merged istanbul coverage. Execution is owner-initiated per DEBT-465's four parts. The Active register is DEBT-337, DEBT-414, DEBT-464, and DEBT-465. Next Debt ID is DEBT-466.

**Earlier 2026-08-13 update:** **The DEBT-414 provider-owned template inventory is COMPLETE and every enabled template is transactional.** The owner reviewed the live Stripe dashboard and the production Clerk instance with CLI/API corroboration. Stripe sends eight customer email types: the trial-end reminder, upcoming-renewal, expiring-card, failed-card-payment, and failed-bank-debit emails, finalized invoices and credit notes as one toggle-governed type, and the previously off successful-payment and refund receipts the owner enabled; the one-time trial-over statement-descriptor message is card-statement text, not an email. **2026-08-10 update:** **DEBT-464 filed and adversarially corrected: Vercel Web Analytics activation with privacy-policy coherence.** Authenticated Vercel REST evidence confirms the project toggle has been on since 2026-02-24. The measured application baseline has no `@vercel/analytics` integration, and signed-out production HTML for four public pages embeds no analytics script. A direct insights asset returns 200, but that result proves neither why the route is available nor that a page uses it. The former unnamed 404 probe did not prove the absence of a query API or dataset. Vercel now documents both Web Analytics API and CLI queries; the official CLI returned no page-view groups for the observable 31-day Hobby window but could not query back to the toggle date, so all-history claims remain UNVERIFIABLE. The audit also found that the commercial subscription product is on Vercel Hobby, that a bare root component would expose identifier-bearing application URLs, that the existing same-origin referrer policy would bypass event-payload redaction, and that the plan omitted the promised collection-point notice. The corrected activation gate requires an owner-authorized Pro upgrade, a fail-closed four-route allowlist, query and fragment removal from event payloads, origin-only request referrers, a visible footer notice, and complete collection and retention disclosures. The implementation records production evidence as pending; a docs-only closeout adds browser-network proof and a path-grouped official CLI result after deployment. Terms version/hash and pricing disclosure version remain untouched; the CAN-SPAM owner ruling narrows but does not close the provider-owned template inventory. Next Debt ID is DEBT-465.

**Prior 2026-08-03 update:** **Promo #724 review round applied: 11 CodeRabbit findings on the dev→main deploying diff, all documentation, zero on the shipped code.** The critical one redesigned the specced setup-mode consent flow — create the Stripe setup Session **without** `customer` (supplying one auto-attaches the PaymentMethod at completion, before webhook validation, breaking fail-closed) and attach explicitly in the verified webhook path. Others: same-transaction acknowledgment outbox; price-change replay safety (operation key, Stripe reconciliation, `proration_behavior: 'none'`); ToS trial term now names and forbids the delete-and-recreate pathway; price-change notices must include cancellation instructions (Cal. § 17602); privacy policy no longer lists card brand/last4 the app never receives; incident procedure gains an out-of-band contact, full § 899-aa regulator payload, and the statutory credential-breach delivery condition; security inventory adds GitHub/DNS/CI control-plane rows; retention review now enumerates all six open-ended policies (was "two"). Same-day earlier update: **DEBT-414 Terms of Service drafted and adversarially reviewed.** `docs/legal/terms-of-service.md` now exists — authored in house from the codified statutes and codebase facts (prices, 7-day no-card trial, cancel-at-period-end via the app's Billing page → Stripe portal, past-due grace semantics), with internal Decisions-on-record (no arbitration clause; cancel-forward refunds with two owner-adverse carve-outs; no indemnification; email-only notice channel) and a Provenance appendix. An independent adversarial review produced 12 findings, all applied — the critical one: the draft's original 5-business-day material-change notice floor could yield 6 calendar days, violating California § 17602(g)(2)'s 7-calendar-day fee-change floor while the provenance table certified compliance; the window is now ≥7 calendar days AND ≥5 business days, ≤30 days, anchored to each subscriber's effectiveness renewal. NY § 527-a pin-cites corrected to subdivision 1; the CodeRabbit round-3 review of the same branch was resolved earlier the same day (two parsed-DOM/hyphen nits fixed, the NY § 527-a Major rebutted on-thread from the codified statute — it relied on the superseded original S3008 amendment). The final-coherence review preserves the original four blocker categories, moves blocker 1 from "ToS absent" to "ToS drafted, pending owner read-through + deferred legal review," and adds a fifth blocker because the destination mailbox provider and transactional sender are not yet identified. Next Debt ID remains DEBT-463. Earlier 2026-07-29 update: **DEBT-414 adversarial legal-copy and implementation-spec audit completed. The original Privacy Policy draft contained publication-blocking factual absolutes about 24-hour deletion, account-deletion scope, Sentry, analytics, payment information, cookies, security, and processing location; those statements are corrected in the draft and its provenance table now records CONFIRMED / REFUTED / UNVERIFIABLE results. The Terms of Service remains undrafted by instruction. The active work is no longer a half-day page build: ROSCA, California ARL, and New York GBL § 527-a require pre-billing visual-proximity disclosure, affirmative consent, durable evidence/acknowledgment, notices, and simple cancellation; New York's SHIELD Act also requires an operational safeguards and incident-response program. Six direct providers are recorded (Clerk, Stripe, Neon, Vercel, Sentry, ImprovMX). **Terminal-close disposition rule:** confirmed P3-or-higher findings enter Active as must-fix; confirmed P4 findings enter Parked (accepted-risk) and do not extend a mandatory fix wave.

**Stable baseline (2026-07-18):** the bug register has zero active must-fix bugs; every remaining active or terminal-close parked item was re-verified against the current tree on this date, and future sweeps are owner-initiated rather than self-perpetuating. The separately deferred optimization table retains its own revive-trigger policy.

**Direction review (2026-07-19 — Wave A):** DEBT-443/444/448/449 now carry binding per-part forest-review verdicts: prefer compatibility discipline, pure normalization, existing seams, single-statement SQL, and explicit policy names over version frameworks, new schedulers, archive ledgers, or speculative cleanup methods. Cluster cleanup ruling: no generalized maintenance cron; `idempotency_keys` and `rate_limits` retain their existing request-path owners but move to bounded one-statement `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` deletes; processed Stripe events retain the successful-webhook owner; the inline `user.deleted` path owns immediate successful customer cleanup and the daily drain owns retries/fallback. Retention is table/state-specific — the existing 24-hour idempotency TTL, a target 24-hour rate-limit horizon, and 90-day processed Stripe events; unresolved Stripe and handled Clerk events currently lack a repo-owned terminal retention policy, so the chosen fix names their retain-until-resolution/retain policies — while automatic Clerk pruning is parked until two consecutive monthly production censuses meet its measured growth trigger; DEBT-449 is re-tiered P3 → P4 because its speculative growth mechanism is parked and only policy/DRY plus completion-bookkeeping work survives.

**Direction review (2026-07-20 — Wave B):** DEBT-445/446/447 now carry binding per-part deploy/operator-safety verdicts: deploy-time drift detection belongs to the repository build path, using one extracted DEBT-442 verifier and allowlist before/after migration with E2E as a second consumer, while CI separately pins journal monotonicity; provider Preview Branching and a disposable restore exercise are named OWNER-GATED steps, not assumed repo state. All migrate/seed/studio entry points share one redacted `host[:port]/database` target classifier/serializer: implicit fallback is refused, every human REMOTE invocation acknowledges the canonical JSON array of sorted unique redacted targets, and only checked-in CI/Vercel/resolver wrappers can call a non-user-selectable managed mode; `db:seed:all` uses the existing named Production resolution only as an exclusion fence and executes non-production targets, while dedicated Production seed resolves that named identity itself and uses the same consent boundary. Per-question plan/freshness machinery is parked until a stale-corpus incident or second independent writer. Application sessions take conservative 30-second statement, 5-second lock, and 60-second idle-transaction bounds; the statement bound intentionally trails the verified 10–15-second non-cancelling user timers; postgres.js `max: 10` becomes explicit; migration-session timeout machinery is parked pending proof on Drizzle Kit's actual connection, pooled-host fail-closed enforcement is accepted as disproportionate, and the vendor page becomes pointers to source rather than copied configuration. No row moves or priority changes were warranted: each doc retains a P3 history-backed operational risk after speculative/provider-only scope is separated.

**Direction review (2026-07-20 — Wave C):** DEBT-450/451/453/454 now carry binding data-access/contract verdicts. Measurement boundary: bounded consumer-shaped waste removal proceeds for history summaries and bookmark width, while finalize, bookmark pagination, lifetime/ranking, and index work are PARKED until the named sampled Sentry spans plus Neon Query Analytics/slow-query or cardinality evidence meet each doc's trigger; tracing remains `0` today, so arithmetic reachability is not treated as measured latency. DB-enforcement posture: ship constraints for live writers or cheap complete invariants, but ACCEPT user↔session and provenance failures that require nonexistent bypass writers, with concrete failure statements and pre-decided hardening; the live dual reader gains divergence warning and the real 23505/fake pair must share production semantics under the DEBT-435/455 and Wave A DEBT-443.3 fake-fidelity law. Contract/seam ruling: consolidate the four effective-selection sites and eleven difficulty encodings (the live pgEnum only with zero generated migration SQL), delete dead `AttemptWriter.deleteById`, document the persisted-schema and `end()` context laws, pin current lock/retry wiring, and PARK stronger lock factories until misuse/new-consumer evidence; no priority changes are warranted because DEBT-450 retains bounded P3 history/bookmark work and the other three remain P4 latent-contract hygiene.

**Direction review (2026-07-21 — Wave D):** DEBT-452/455/456/457/458 now carry binding error-seam, fake-fidelity, and determinacy verdicts. Error-seam map: repositories preserve causes, controllers select the phase-owned primary error, and one bounded projector allowlisting only class/application code/PostgreSQL code/constraint owns both webhook failure ledgers and every raw `{ err }`/`{ error }` log crossing; raw message/detail/query/params/stack/cause text never cross those diagnostic seams, while DEBT-443 retains ownership of cached client-facing error parity. Fake-fidelity requires the real user-upsert ordering/timestamp semantics and guard-passing snapshot mutations with before/after state proof. Determinacy requires end/finalize to preserve its key on thrown outcomes, reason-targeted practice recovery with scoped legacy bare-conflict compatibility, and fresh observation per Stripe CAS attempt; the proven double-retrieve cost is ACCEPTED until its named Stripe/Sentry trigger fires, and no priority changes are warranted because every surviving Wave D item remains P4 hardening.

**Active-row audit:** 2026-06-24 — **DEBT-424** (rate-limit idempotent server actions on cache-miss only, P3) **resolved + archived** to `docs/_archive/debt/` and moved to the Resolved table. Implemented exactly per the decided spec: a `beforeExecute` hook in the shared `withIdempotency`/`executeIdempotent` runs the limiter only on a fresh cache-miss claim, so idempotent replays return the stored success/error instead of `RATE_LIMITED`. `claim()` returns the `claimedAt` token and `abortClaim`/`storeResult`/`storeError` are fenced to that exact still-pending claim — hook-denied `RATE_LIMITED` is neither cached nor left pending, and a stale execution/abort cannot delete or clobber a newer reclaimed row (outcome immutability). All 8 in-scope controllers moved to the hook (behavior-preserving); the 3 no-limiter actions + per-IP webhook route limiters stayed out of scope. Verification: CodeRabbit 0-actionable on the exact head `7ab0de10` (0 unresolved threads) + an independent adversarial concurrency review CLEAN; full local gate green (typecheck, lint, unit, browser, integration, build, e2e). Shipped via PR #512 (squash `40c33e4b` to `dev`) → promo #513 (merge `0861f28a` to `main`, byte-identical-tree owner-override under chronic CodeRabbit rate-limit), prod-verified (`dpl_Dz8UjHFSBgZuTc6fXXkJyrrAEsTR` / `0861f28a`, `addictionboards.com` 200). **Next Debt ID remains DEBT-425.**

**Active-row audit:** 2026-06-12 — filed, re-audited, and implemented **DEBT-417** (multi-clone local test infrastructure isolation, P2) in the BUG-245 branch per owner instruction to combine the DevX fix with the checkout-race PR. The original local harness assumed one clone and shared two global singletons: the database-isolated E2E pre-step `lsof -ti:3000 | xargs kill -9` blanket-killed any `:3000` holder (so clone B SIGKILLed clone A's Next server or a human `pnpm dev` server → phantom `ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED`), and the test-DB container name was a hardcoded `naltrexone-test-db` reused-by-name (silent shared-Postgres corruption; `db:test:up` duplicate-name failure). Implemented fix: deleted the blanket kill, introduced `scripts/resolve-local-test-target.ts` as the single resolver, derived a stable per-worktree instance id with explicit env override, removed `container_name`, used `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME`/`docker compose -p` namespacing, and threaded `PORT`/`NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL`/`DATABASE_URL` through E2E, integration, and `db:test:*` wrappers. Rejected shortcuts: hand-wired offsets, shell `flock` as the default, blanket kills on a derived port, and ephemeral containers as the default. CI is single-clone and remains on its supplied service DB/app path. **DEBT-417 is now resolved + archived** — the implementation shipped in PR #421 (squash `be50dda7`, `main` fast-forwarded), the doc moved to `docs/_archive/debt/`, and the BUG-245 feature branch was deleted. At that point, the next Debt ID was DEBT-418.

> **Terminology correction (2026-08-20):** the historical DEBT-417 phrase “hermetic E2E” meant only that Postgres moved to isolated local Docker. The suite has always called Clerk and Stripe test mode and is therefore database-isolated, not hermetic; DEBT-471 supersedes that label.

## What is Technical Debt?

Technical debt documents known shortcuts, deferred work, and architectural compromises. They serve as:

1. **Visibility** — Make implicit debt explicit
2. **Prioritization** — Help decide what to tackle and when
3. **Tracking** — Ensure debt is paid down over time

| [DEBT-385](../_archive/debt/debt-385-stripe-invoice-event-subscription-ref-schema-drift.md) | Stripe invoice event subscription-reference schema drift — third Stripe follow-up after DEBT-384/386. The app is pinned to Stripe API version `2026-01-28.clover` (`lib/stripe.ts:22`, set in commit `d9f3cbe4` on 2026-01-31), and Clover invoice payloads expose the subscription reference at the nested `data.object.parent.subscription_details.subscription` with root `data.object.subscription: null`. Before the fix, `stripeEventWithSubscriptionRefSchema` was a direct alias of the root-only `stripeCheckoutSessionSchema`, so `getSubscriptionUpdateForSubscriptionRefEvent` found no reference on invoice events and returned `undefined` — a silent 200 no-op, masked in practice by Stripe's parallel `customer.subscription.updated` events. Fix shipped in PR #312, adapter-scoped only: added `stripeInvoiceSubscriptionRefSchema` (nested `parent.subscription_details.subscription` fragment, `.passthrough()`-preserving) merged into `stripeEventWithSubscriptionRefSchema`, plus an `extractSubscriptionRef` helper that prefers the nested ref over the legacy root ref (`payload.parent?.subscription_details?.subscription ?? payload.subscription ?? null`); the processor swapped its one-line `payload.subscription` read for `extractSubscriptionRef(payload)` (`src/adapters/gateways/stripe/stripe-webhook-processor.ts`). Strict TDD: RED commit `54161954` added 299 lines of failing tests (zero production code) across `stripe-webhook-schemas.test.ts` (new), `stripe-webhook-processor.test.ts`, and `stripe-payment-gateway.test.ts`; GREEN commit `6f3443ff` added the schema + helper + wiring. Coverage spans all four extraction cases — nested-only, legacy root-only, both-present (prefers nested), and neither (intentional no-op) — plus an expanded object-shaped ref case and a passthrough-semantics guard added in response to CodeRabbit. `lib/stripe.ts:22` API pin deliberately left untouched (out of scope). CodeRabbit APPROVED on latest head `f6b76411` with no actionable comments; merge state CLEAN; hosted test, Vercel, and codecov/patch all passed. main and dev fast-forwarded to `6157bb1b`. | P2 | 2026-05-20 | [PR #312](https://github.com/The-Obstacle-Is-The-Way/naltrexone-university/pull/312) |
| [DEBT-386](../_archive/debt/debt-386-e2e-stripe-customer-ownership-drift-webhook-500s.md) | E2E Stripe customer ownership drift causing webhook 500s — second-layer follow-up to DEBT-384. Root cause confirmed post-DEBT-384: shared E2E Stripe test customer and active subscription were being rebound across independent DB-local user IDs because `tests/e2e/helpers/seed-test-user.ts` selected the first customer matching the seed email regardless of which environment created it, and rewrote `metadata.user_id` on any reused active subscription. The dev-preview webhook then received `customer.subscription.updated` events whose `metadata.user_id` pointed at a user row from a different database, and `DrizzleStripeCustomerRepository.insert` correctly rejected the rebind via `ApplicationError('CONFLICT', 'Stripe customer id is already mapped to a different user')` → HTTP 500 → Stripe retry loop. Two-layer fix shipped in PR #311 mirroring DEBT-384's marker pattern: **T2** scoped the E2E seeder by explicit owner metadata via `E2E_STRIPE_OWNER` (`tests/e2e/helpers/seed-test-user.ts:82-93` requires it when Stripe creds are real, defaults to `local-dev` only against the `sk_test_dummy` placeholder; `findOwnerMatchedStripeCustomer` and `listOwnerMatchedStripeSubscriptions` filter by `metadata.e2e_owner`; metadata repair only fires for same-owner subscriptions). **T3** added a webhook-controller-only owner-mismatch skip: the normalizer (`src/adapters/gateways/stripe/stripe-subscription-normalizer.ts`) stamps a typed `STRIPE_ERROR` with `fieldErrors: { 'metadata.e2e_owner': ['mismatch'] }` when `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_E2E_OWNER` is configured and the inbound `metadata.e2e_owner` differs; `processStripeWebhook` (`src/adapters/controllers/stripe-webhook-controller.ts:86-91`) catches ONLY that specific tuple via `isE2EOwnerMismatchEvent` (`src/adapters/shared/stripe-subscription-errors.ts:22-32`), logs `reason: 'e2e_owner_mismatch'`, returns 200 without claiming the event in `stripe_events`. Webhook-vs-reconcile asymmetry preserved: `reconcileStripeSubscriptions` receives the same optional owner configuration but stays fail-closed — a configured owner mismatch becomes a row failure, not a skip (regression test in `reconcile-stripe-subscriptions.test.ts`). Configuration surface: `.env.example` documents both variables; `.github/workflows/ci.yml:62` sets `E2E_STRIPE_OWNER=github-ci`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml:168` enforces `require_non_empty E2E_STRIPE_OWNER` in the E2E credential gate (added in response to CodeRabbit on the final review cycle); `lib/env.ts` accepts optional `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_E2E_OWNER`; `lib/container/gateways.ts` threads it through constructor injection to the Stripe payment gateway. Four CodeRabbit review cycles, ending APPROVED on `90008b95` after the E2E owner gate validation, pagination, and prior nits were addressed. Full local quality gate green: typecheck, lint (19 pre-existing oversized-file warnings only), 2456 unit, 265 browser, 97 integration, build, `E2E_STRIPE_OWNER=local-dev pnpm test:e2e` 35/35; hosted CI test, CodeRabbit, Vercel, codecov/patch all SUCCESS. Post-merge ops status verified complete on 2026-05-19: Vercel Preview `(dev)` has `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_E2E_OWNER=vercel-dev-preview` and the dev-preview alias points at redeployed build `[redacted deployment id]`; both test-mode and live Stripe endpoints include `customer.subscription.created`; live mode has zero undelivered events. Test mode still has historical pre-fix undelivered events, newest `2026-05-16T16:46:46Z`, with no post-fix accumulation. No required residual work remains; stale non-owner test subscription cleanup is optional dashboard hygiene only. | P2 | 2026-05-16 | [PR #311](https://github.com/The-Obstacle-Is-The-Way/naltrexone-university/pull/311) |
| [DEBT-384](../_archive/debt/debt-384-stripe-webhook-error-rate-investigation.md) | Stripe webhook delivery error-rate investigation — root cause confirmed empirically via Stripe CLI (sandboxes = consolidated test mode on the project's John-Jung Stripe account) + independent adversarial audit: E2E test helper `tests/e2e/helpers/seed-test-user.ts` created Stripe subscriptions and customers WITHOUT `metadata.user_id`, which made `normalizeStripeSubscriptionUpdate` (`src/adapters/gateways/stripe/stripe-subscription-normalizer.ts:28-46`) throw `STRIPE_ERROR`, returning HTTP 500 `{"error":"Webhook processing failed"}` and triggering Stripe's automatic retry loop → 6 visible failed deliveries from 2 unique phantom subscriptions on the redacted E2E customer (one canceled after 90.004-day sandbox auto-retention, one active). Two-layer fix shipped in PR #310: **T2** patched seed-test-user.ts to write `metadata.user_id` on customer + subscription create, AND added a self-healing repair path that patches missing metadata on any pre-existing active E2E subscription (so the two phantom subs auto-repair on next E2E run — no Stripe Dashboard cleanup needed). **T3** added a webhook-controller-only log-and-skip path: the normalizer now stamps `fieldErrors: { 'metadata.user_id': ['required'] }` on its existing throw, and `processStripeWebhook` (`src/adapters/controllers/stripe-webhook-controller.ts:64-84`) catches ONLY that specific tuple via `isMissingStripeSubscriptionUserIdError` (`src/adapters/shared/stripe-subscription-errors.ts`), emits a structured `metadata_missing` warning, and returns without claiming the event in `stripe_events`. ## Debt Statuses

- **Open** — Debt acknowledged, not yet addressed
- **In Progress** — Actively being paid down
- **Resolved** — Debt paid, verified
- **Accepted** — Intentionally kept (with justification)

## Priority Levels

- **P0** — Critical: Blocks development or production
- **P1** — High: Significant impact on velocity or quality
- **P2** — Medium: Noticeable friction, should address soon
- **P3** — Low: Minor inconvenience
- **P4** — Trivial: Nice to clean up

---

## How to Document New Debt

1. Create `debt-NNN-short-description.md` using the template below
2. Set status to "Open"
3. Assign priority based on impact
4. Submit PR for review

## Resolution Evidence Conventions

- A resolution that deletes a pattern lands with the executable guard that keeps the pattern deleted.
- A scheduled-only proof is not claimed as complete until a hosted run has actually executed it; local or unpublished timing is not a hosted receipt.
- A guard lands with a red test for the exact forbidden state it claims to reject.

## Debt Template

```markdown
# DEBT-NNN: Short Title

> Close using [the archive convention](../../AGENTS.md#closing-and-archiving-documentation-records).

**Status:** Open | In Progress | Resolved | Accepted
**Priority:** P0 | P1 | P2 | P3 | P4
**Date:** YYYY-MM-DD
**Resolved:** YYYY-MM-DD | —
**Verification receipts:** Promoted implementation and verification links | —

---

## Description

What is the debt? Why does it exist?

## Impact

How does this affect development, quality, or users?

## Resolution

What needs to be done to pay down this debt?

## Verification

How will we verify the debt is resolved?

## Related

- Links to code, specs, ADRs
```

---
